Move local prediction application to AsyncContext::ApplyPredictionsTo()

This CL extracts the heuristic-reset and prediction-application lambdas
out of AutofillManager::UpdateFormCache().

This improves the status quo because there's no direct link between
Apply and Reset, and it happened in the past that CLs would add logic to
Apply without remembering to add logic to Reset.

This also fixes a bug in setting AutofillField::supported_ml_types_,
although the Autofill ML model is currently unused.

This CL is part of a chain aiming at simplifying the parsing logic and
reusing AM::UpdateFormCache() in server predictions. Overall direction
can be found in go/autofill-old-new-parsing-architecture.

[Public Transit] Check the target is in view in HtmlElement triggers

HtmlElement#clickTo() and #longPressTo() now assert, after scrolling
the element into view, that its client rect is not empty (e.g.
display: none or scale(0)) and that its center is inside the visual
viewport, instead of tapping outside the page (or on browser controls)
and timing out later. The visual viewport is used because scrolling
can move it instead of the layout viewport on Desktop Android.

The check and the tap use the element's client rect, which includes CSS
transforms, like HtmlElement's inViewport option. The offset-based
bounds used before missed e.g. the translateY(-100%) of the bottom link
in top-bottom-links.html.

DOMUtils#clickNodeByJs(), only used by HtmlElement#clickTo(), now does
the check. Add DOMUtils#longPressNodeInViewByJs() for #longPressTo();
longPressNodeByJs() is unchanged since longPressNode() and its many
callers use it.

Follow-up to crrev.com/c/8775468.

[build] Enable auto-restat-content on build/Siso builders

Enable the Siso auto-restat-content experiment on chromium.build and
chromium.build.fyi builders so restat rules use content-based restat
unless opted out with restat_content = False.

This validates auto-restat-content across all platforms before making
it the default in Siso.

[remoting] Migrate to Value string view getters

Moves remoting/ from DictValue::FindString() and Value::GetString() /
GetIfString() to FindStr(), GetStr() and GetIfStr(), which return
std::optional<std::string_view> and std::string_view.

Lookups are rewritten into a few fixed shapes: ASSIGN_OR_RETURN for an
early return, value_or() / transform() for defaults, a
std::optional<std::string_view> declared in the `if` when the value is
only used in that block, and in tests EXPECT_EQ on the optional or
ASSERT_OK_AND_ASSIGN.

Callees that took a `const std::string&` take a std::string_view
instead, and containers get the view through emplace_back(). That
includes three virtuals, HeartbeatSender::Delegate::OnUpdateHostOwner(),
OAuthClient::GetCredentialsFromAuthCode() and It2MeHost::Connect(), and
what they forward to: GetCanonicalEmail() and, in google_apis/gaia,
gaia::GaiaOAuthClient::GetTokensFromAuthCode(). 2 call sites in
production code now copy into a std::string, both mojo calls. 2 of the
68 files are pure renames.

IceConfig::Parse() logged the pointer of an invalid lifetimeDuration
and logs the string now.

RemoteWebAuthnNativeMessagingHost::FindRequestDataOrSendError()
returns a pointer into the dict and moves in a separate CL.

tree
f59aeddfdad8ec24936a2966e14895fe299d544a
parent
45032a12e30bcf94540d5fabc6282e68cc5c983f [ diff ] 
Harden RenderWidgetHostViewAura popup teardown and null-window access

This CL ensures that popup views in RenderWidgetHostViewAura handle
deferred destruction and pre-initialization states safely. That includes
- unlinking popup parent/child pointers in OnDestroyOrDefer()
- guarding Shutdown() and bounds accessors/mutators when teardown is
  pending or window_ is null

It also derives IsHTMLFormPopup() from WidgetType::kPopup to be more
robust against unlinked parents.

🌺 Revert "Roll Android Lint from EMvaA_bqOEaSua9qe... to _nuy0Xbr090BrDtPo..."

This reverts commit e54ffe0efb4aa65177a53db073a333d477ad9420.
Culprit: e54ffe0efb4a - "Roll Android Lint from EMvaA_bqOEaSua9qe... to _nuy0Xbr090BrDtPo..." (refs/heads/main@{#1755428}, https://crrev.com/c/8870190)

Reason for revert: consistently breaks Android lint compilation on
android-desktop-x64-archive-rel and android_compile_dbg (1/1 builds on
android-desktop-x64-archive-rel since landing, plus breaking Java CQ
builds).

Mechanism: Rolling chromium/third_party/android_build_tools/lint in DEPS
introduced a new Lint binary that rejects @IntDef
(@LensMetrics.LensSupportStatus) on boxed Integer paired with JSpecify
@Nullable at
chrome/browser/lens/java/src/org/chromium/chrome/browser/lens/LensSupportStatusHelper.java:36
("Error: This annotation does not apply for type Integer; expected int
[SupportAnnotationUsage]" in ACTION
//chrome/android:chrome_public_apk__lint).

Example failing runs:
https://ci.chromium.org/ui/p/chromium/builders/ci/android-desktop-x64-archive-rel/23709
https://ci.chromium.org/b/8664089000088586481

Reverting to green the tree and restore CQ throughput. Please re-land
once LensSupportStatusHelper.java or the lint check is fixed.

1c644e8c2ff2a1722ef10874da332cfa14f189ce [ diff ] 
[Actor] Pass ActorKeyedService to onTaskCompleted

ActorBackgroundActuationManager.onTaskCompleted() looked up the
completed task through ProfileManager.getLastUsedRegularProfile(),
which can pick the wrong profile when multiple profiles are loaded.

We now pass the observed ActorKeyedService from
ActorForegroundServiceManager through ActorForegroundServiceController
to ActorBackgroundActuationManager.onTaskCompleted(), removing its
ProfileManager dependency and the stale ProfileManager test override.

No behaviour change for single-profile users.

Enable gn check --check-generated on win-rel

Set gn_check_generated on ci/Win x64 Builder, so the compile step of
win-rel and the other try builders that mirror it runs
"gn check --check-generated" on the compiled targets and fails on
violations. ci/GPU Win x64 Builder sets the same value, since
win-rel mirrors both and mirrored specs must match.

The check passes on all compiled targets with this config in led builds
of the affected builders:
- ci/Win x64 Builder: https://ci.chromium.org/b/8664098834506869345
- ci/GPU Win x64 Builder: https://ci.chromium.org/b/8664098832601547777
- ci/win-devtools-frontend-rel: https://ci.chromium.org/b/8664098830769330593
- ci/win-separate-renderer-fyi-rel: https://ci.chromium.org/b/8664098828985787473
- ci/win-webdriver-bidi-rel: https://ci.chromium.org/b/8664098827192396801
- try/win-rel: https://ci.chromium.org/b/8664098825426555553
- try/win-devtools-frontend-rel: https://ci.chromium.org/b/8664098823585483217
- try/win-separate-renderer-rel: https://ci.chromium.org/b/8664098821725342913
- try/win-webdriver-bidi-rel: https://ci.chromium.org/b/8664098819862959425
- try/win11-rel: https://ci.chromium.org/b/8664098818355626609
- try/win_chromium_x64_rel_ng: https://ci.chromium.org/b/8664098816486717921
- try/windows-no-initial-webui-rel: https://ci.chromium.org/b/8664098814604444337

Enable gn check --check-generated on android-arm64-rel

Set gn_check_generated on ci/android-14-arm64-rel, so the compile step
of android-arm64-rel and the other try builders that mirror it runs
"gn check --check-generated" on the compiled targets and fails on
violations. ci/Android Release (Pixel 2) sets the same value, since
android-arm64-rel mirrors both and mirrored specs must match.

The check passes on all compiled targets with this config in led builds
of the affected builders:
- ci/android-14-arm64-rel: https://ci.chromium.org/b/8664095452622912449
- ci/Android Release (Pixel 2): https://ci.chromium.org/b/8664095410999476369
- try/android-arm64-rel: https://ci.chromium.org/b/8664095403753823873
- try/android-14-arm64-rel: https://ci.chromium.org/b/8664095407649606657
- try/gpu-try-android-pixel-2-64: https://ci.chromium.org/b/8664095401172073425

Thu Nov 26 07:23:29 2026
tree
208ae6ddb090d98f2ed71ee3d53955c522b7e268
parent
0e7ac46d21a4e7a3e0e9d17326ea204c71ece053 [ diff ] 
Make ShowCreatedPopupWidgetInterceptor safe when popup widget closes

This is the first CL in a chain to eliminate orphaned HTML popups.
Today, orphaned HTML popups can be created if the renderer requests
showing a new HTML popup while the previous one is still showing. In
that case, the browser unlinks the old popup from its parent, but does
not actually shut it down - it remains and can obscure UI. Worse,
RenderWidgetHostViewAura::IsHTMLFormPopup() of an orphaned popup will
return "false" because the check depends on the existence of a linked
parent.

This CL just makes some test preparations to keep the main CL smaller.
Concretely, it allows ShowCreatedPopupWidgetInterceptor to safely
outlive the popup widget.

70279a6ba1bc0a66b4d5c99bcdc5710597a52b1d [ diff ] 
Document the Apple linker opt-in for iOS builds

use_lld = false already makes iOS arm64 builds link with the Apple
linker (ld-prime) without any other build change, as on macOS. Document
it as an opt-in for local arm64 simulator builds on ARM Macs, and note
that arm64e always uses the Apple linker.

Link time of the raw link command for chrome and ios_chrome_unittests,
iOS simulator arm64, non-component, median of 5 runs on an M4 Max:
ld-prime is 4.5x-5.0x faster than LLD with a cold file cache and
1.8x-3.1x faster with a warm one, across debug and release configs.

3c8c984a99b43007bd2a332df6cd761bfeb4d957
parent
29f2b219f62eac765ff8af417108e6b3072ca56a [ diff ] 
[DevTools] Keep worker network conditions until worker terminates

Network.emulateNetworkConditionsByRule on a dedicated worker session had
no effect on the fetch() requests of the worker. The renderer set the
throttling token of the owner frame on these requests, and only while
the Network domain was enabled in the worker.

Also, when the owner frame navigates, the browser detaches the worker
sessions at ReadyToCommitNavigation. The worker continues to run until
the renderer terminates it, or freezes it for the back/forward cache. In
this interval, the requests of the worker ignored the emulated
conditions.

This CL makes these changes:
- Add URLLoaderFactoryParams.devtools_throttling_profile_id. If it is
  set, the network service uses it and ignores the token of the request.
- The browser sets it to the DevTools token of the dedicated worker for
  the subresource factory of the worker.
- Before the forced detach, the worker agent host (including nested
  workers and workers of descendant local roots) takes the network
  conditions of its sessions. It clears them when the worker
  disconnects, when a new session of the same root session attaches,
  when the root session of the client that set them is disposed, or when
  the owning frame is restored from the back/forward cache.
- Network.emulateNetworkConditions on a dedicated worker session now
  fails in the browser before it applies the conditions. Before, the
  browser applied them and the renderer returned "Not supported".

Behavior change: network conditions that a client sets on the page
session no longer apply to the subresource requests of dedicated
workers. The client must set them on the worker session.
offline-dedicated-worker.js is updated for this.

Bug: 590377039
Test: third_party/blink/tools/run_web_tests.py -t Default http/tests/inspector-protocol/network/network-conditions-by-rule-dedicated-worker-navigation.js http/tests/inspector-protocol/network/network-conditions-by-rule-dedicated-worker-bfcache.js http/tests/inspector-protocol/network/network-conditions-by-rule-dedicated-worker-root-session-dispose.js http/tests/inspector-protocol/network/network-conditions-dedicated-worker-session.js http/tests/inspector-protocol/network/offline-dedicated-worker.js
Test: services_unittests --gtest_filter=NetworkServiceTestWithService.FactoryDevToolsThrottlingProfileTakesPrecedence

Encode packed YUV frames from the readback pool
📋 Metadata

21a813e7997608e852c4399efccd23bfb47a54a9
[ log ]
author
Zhibo Wang <zhibo1.wang@intel.com>

Thu Nov 26 07:06:54 2026
tree
6f55ae273a3fa465005a30777b80eecfd349527b
parent
e29b14e025c161f5a64c675855643a5644a6c77d [ diff ] 
Encode packed YUV frames from the readback pool

The readback frame pool hands Y210 and Y410 frames to the D3D12 encoder
as shared memory mappable SharedImages. The encoder treated every such
frame as CPU memory and tried to wrap it as planar YUV, which is not
possible for packed formats, so the encode failed with a conversion
error. These frames are written by the GPU and the SharedImage already
has a D3D11 texture behind it, so resolve that texture in the GPU
process like any other SharedImage frame instead of reading memory.
Frames with a native DXGI handle still open the handle directly, and the
memory upload path is unchanged for every other format.

Bug: 537818862

Revert "scheduler: Add HighPriorityInternalMediaTaskType for foreground pages"

This reverts commit 342d0f0c67a0b9a9a8fb9c7f242d4f6991c7925d.

Reason for revert:
LUCI Bisection has identified this change as the cause of a test failure. See the analysis: https://ci.chromium.org/ui/p/chromium/bisection/test-analysis/b/5174940929097728

Sample build with failed test: https://ci.chromium.org/b/8664111911256493169
Affected test(s):
[://\:blink_web_tests!webtest::fast/mediacapturefromelement#CanvasCaptureMediaStream-creation.html](https://ci.chromium.org/ui/test/chromium/:%2F%2F%5C:blink_web_tests%21webtest::fast%2Fmediacapturefromelement%23CanvasCaptureMediaStream-creation.html?q=VHash%3A0122b06a4371fa09)

If this is a false positive, please report it at http://b.corp.google.com/createIssue?component=1199205&description=Analysis%3A+https%3A%2F%2Fci.chromium.org%2Fui%2Fp%2Fchromium%2Fbisection%2Ftest-analysis%2Fb%2F5174940929097728&format=PLAIN&priority=P3&title=Wrongly+blamed+https%3A%2F%2Fchromium-review.googlesource.com%2Fc%2Fchromium%2Fsrc%2F%2B%2F8563720&type=BUG

Original change's description:
> scheduler: Add HighPriorityInternalMediaTaskType for foreground pages
>
> Currently, TaskType::kInternalMedia and TaskType::kInternalMediaRealTime
> use default PausableTaskQueueTraits with PrioritisationType::kRegular,
> scheduling internal media control tasks and Mojo IPC callbacks at
> kNormalPriority behind main-thread script execution during player
> initialization.
>
> Add the HighPriorityInternalMediaTaskType feature (disabled by default
> for Finch evaluation) and PrioritisationType::kInternalMedia so
> TaskType::kInternalMedia and TaskType::kInternalMediaRealTime run at
> TaskPriority::kHighPriority for visible non-ad main frames, same-origin
> subframes, and important cross-origin subframes, and drop back to
> TaskPriority::kNormalPriority otherwise.
>
> Bug: 573058978
> Change-Id: I5c289d86772ee064980a516155e1944775ce6e9c
> Reviewed-on: https://chromium-review.googlesource.com/c/chromium/src/+/8563720

> Reviewed-by: Dale Curtis <dalecurtis@chromium.org>
> Commit-Queue: Vikram Pasupathy <vpasupathy@chromium.org>
> Cr-Commit-Position: refs/heads/main@{#1755170}

[cast] Disconnect ports on origin change and verify connector origin

Disconnect active API binding ports in cast_api_bindings::Manager when
the tracked origin changes, and guard ScopedApiBinding::SendMessage when
the port is not connected. In CastComponent, reset the connector and
clear the tracked origin on document load events, and set the new origin
only after the page replies with the master port connection message.

Bug: 578684041
Test: cast_components_unittests

[MemoryCoordinator] Add Java TestMemoryConsumerRegistry

Adds a fake memory consumer registry for Robolectric tests, the Java
equivalent of base::TestMemoryConsumerRegistry. It replaces the native
side of MemoryConsumerRegistration, so tests of Java MemoryConsumers can
call notifyReleaseMemory() / notifyUpdateMemoryLimit() directly instead
of mocking JNI.

post-MES gating.

parent
640a942a7a9366941270979a51dd66429c0715a2 [ diff ] 
[webui] Query bundled code cache IDs in renderer

Switch `RendererBlinkPlatformImpl::GetWebUIBundledCodeCacheResourceId()`
to query `ContentRendererClient::GetWebUIBundledCodeCacheResourceId()`
synchronously in the renderer instead of reading the runtime map sent by
the browser over Mojo IPC.

Append `--enable-webui-bundled-code-cache` in
`ChromeContentBrowserClient::AppendExtraCommandLineSwitches()` for WebUI
renderers that disallow V8 feature flag overrides so
`ChromeContentRendererClient` preserves process-level eligibility gating
while eliminating startup ordering races with background module loads.

A follow-up CL removes the now-unused
`SetWebUIResourceUrlToCodeCacheMap` Mojo IPC and browser-side map
construction.

Bug: 402625343, 375509504
Test: unit_tests --gtest_filter=*ChromeContentRendererClient*
Test: browser_tests --gtest_filter=*TabSearchUIBundledCodeCache*:*WebUiNtpBundledCodeCache*

tree
76c2f9aa593b99bbd3406970165bec453648dec3
parent
6aecf94a760ca8bed04f35b1b19554c95653b8b8 [ diff ] 
Restrict ChromeOS manifest URL overrides to component extensions

Update manifest feature definitions for `chrome_url_overrides.keyboard`
and `chrome_url_overrides.activationmessage` in `_manifest_features.json`
to require component extension locations.

These URL overrides represent internal ChromeOS system interfaces
intended for component extensions such as the virtual keyboard.
Adding the component location requirement ensures non-component
extensions cannot declare these overrides during manifest parsing.

In addition, an override entry may already be stored in the
`extensions.chrome_url_overrides` profile pref, registered by an older
browser version that allowed the override. Such an entry is now
ignored when resolving chrome:// URLs unless the extension still
declares an override for the page, and it is removed when overrides are
validated at startup.

TAG=agy

tree
ca32529e0899cbfc664604c81a26d178239a5fec
parent
ac7b81db023a00101ac257de1ab75bd699f93505 [ diff ] 
Copy shared buffer views in Blink Fetch BytesConsumers

BytesConsumer::BeginRead() and DOMArrayBufferView::ByteSpan() expect
unshared memory whose contents do not change while downstream consumers
read or decode the returned byte span. Previously, FormDataBytesConsumer
called view->ByteSpan() unconditionally on DOMArrayBufferView inputs,
and ReadableStreamBytesConsumer retained MaybeShared<DOMUint8Array>
chunks directly without copying shared backing stores or handling
resizable/growable buffer views.

Update FormDataBytesConsumer to check DOMArrayBufferView::IsShared() in
CreateEncodedFormData() and copy shared views via
RelaxedAtomicReadMemcpy() into an owned Vector<char> for
EncodedFormData. Update ReadableStreamBytesConsumer to reject
resizable/growable views via IGNORE_EXCEPTION, copy shared Uint8Array
chunks into unshared memory using RelaxedAtomicReadMemcpy(), and verify
CHECK(!pending_buffer_->IsShared()) in BeginRead().

0240cbb2f8cc2fb992b946d97410074a26a951d5

5c682596d8b2c908d1dd33495719bb8ccae154aa [ diff ] 
Revert "Reland "Avoid anti-aliasing straight edges in CustomCornersBackground""

This reverts commit a1dd4bd1b5af8845a09144d3a1a0d710681d93a8.

Reason for revert: With a theme that has a toolbar image (such as
Morpheon Dark), the toolbar image is painted in device space, so at
fractional scale factors it is shrunk and covers only part of the
toolbar (https://crbug.com/590497358).

Original change's description:
> Reland "Avoid anti-aliasing straight edges in CustomCornersBackground"
>
> This is a reland of commit a61e461835d7a50fef094f1d539355847d635c36.
>
> The original change replaced a single anti-aliased rrect draw with four
> anti-aliased non-convex kDifference clips plus a non-AA rect fill. The
> clips force clip masks and regressed first contentful paint in
> system_health.common_desktop (https://crbug.com/589018791).
>
> This reland keeps the single anti-aliased rrect draw instead. On
> non-pixel canvases, where the recording matrix is a pure translation and
> raster applies the device scale factor, the rrect bounds are rounded to
> device pixels in layer space and the rrect is drawn in device space.
> The straight edges then land on pixel boundaries, so their AA produces
> no partial alpha, while the corners stay anti-aliased. Other cases
> (pixel canvases, which already snap view bounds, and non-translation
> transforms) draw the plain AA rrect as before the original change.
>
> The regression test is skipped when the glass frame is enabled (macOS
> 26+ with the GlassFrame field trial). There the browser frame view
> paints to its own layer, so OnNativeWidgetPaint() does not paint the
> toolbar and the test failed on the Mac-26 and Mac-27 CI builders.
>
> Original change's description:
> > Avoid anti-aliasing straight edges in CustomCornersBackground
> >
> > CustomCornersBackground::Paint drew the view's entire bounding rounded
> > rect with anti-aliasing enabled. At fractional scale factors on
> > non-pixel-canvas platforms (such as Linux with fractional text scaling),
> > anti-aliasing the straight edges of the local bounds produces partial
> > alpha along the boundary pixel row when a scaled edge does not land on
> > an integer pixel. Adjacent views or childless solid-color separator
> > layers snap their edges to the nearest integer pixel, leaving a 1px
> > semi-transparent seam at the toolbar boundary where the translucent
> > window background or desktop shows through.
> >
> > When the corner radii fit within the view bounds, clip out only the
> > rounded corners with anti-aliasing and paint the rectangular bounds
> > without anti-aliasing so straight edges snap to integer pixels.
> >
> > Bug: 503507695
> > Change-Id: Ie8bbdaae186aeb7cfbb962b2bf1c1f7f1ba04330
> > Reviewed-on: https://chromium-review.googlesource.com/c/chromium/src/+/8812433
> > Commit-Queue: Thomas Anderson <thomasanderson@chromium.org>
> > Reviewed-by: Mike Wasserman <msw@chromium.org>
> > Cr-Commit-Position: refs/heads/main@{#1751146}
>
> Bug: 503507695
> Change-Id: Idb8180a851780324afdd2b185cd36065ac801931
> Fixed: 589018791
> Cq-Include-Trybots: luci.chromium.try:mac26-arm64-rel

08732b003d9a077ada8aeb285a862e9a6a2a5da8 [ diff ] 
Merge & inline {Should,Can}RouteToNativeMojoEngine().

ShouldRouteToNativeMojoEngine() in NativeInputMethodEngine and
NativeInputMethodEngineObserver are effectively identical at
runtime. They both wrap CanRouteToNativeMojoEngine and augment it
with consideration for IME Service unavailability in tests.

This refactoring primarily helps reduce the potential confusion
of Should vs Can notions in the routing logic (which concretely
only differs in tests) and facilitate further clarification (re:
somewhat misleading RouteToNativeMojoEngine semantic).

It also has a secondary side effect of avoiding duplicative
helpers across 2 classes that work hand-in-hand with each other.

tree
4a5d369e74dc22254cf634d64f96ed6d5504daec
parent
e0459acfa99afebda36c381d83364b1843d83cdd [ diff ] 
WebUI: Pass ts_library() in_files via a response file

On Windows, a CreateProcess command line is limited to 32767 characters.
The ts_library() action for //chrome/browser/resources/settings:build_ts
has grown past that limit (about 32.9K characters, ~30K of which is the
--in_files list), so any local Windows build of chrome now fails with:

  ninja: fatal: CreateProcess: The parameter is incorrect.
   (is the command line too long?)

Siso local execution fails the same way ("The filename or extension is
too long"). Only remote execution is unaffected, which is why the bots
have not caught it.

On Windows hosts, write in_files to a GN response file and pass its path
to ts_library.py through a new --in_files_file flag. The script reads
the file list from it, so the generated tsconfig is unchanged. This is
keyed on host_os rather than on remote execution, since remote Windows
builds can still fall back to local execution. Other hosts keep using
--in_files.

The Siso ts_library handler parses --in_files from the command line to
compute remote inputs, so it now also reads the file list from the
response file content when --in_files_file is present.

Fix macOS dictionary lookup after the source frame is deleted

The source frame can be deleted while its context menu is still open.
GetRenderFrameHost() then returns null, and invoking Look Up dereferences
it. Return without performing the lookup when the frame no longer exists.

Add a browser regression test that removes the source iframe before
executing the dictionary lookup command.

tree
5f1f8195d339df5f32e250e575954f83298ab7db
parent
50b92fb536147ffebd8d36ea2a781a04a6a9c512 [ diff ] 
OOR-CORS: Remove kSafeRevalidation feature and is_revalidating plumbing

`network::features::kSafeRevalidation` has been enabled by default since
crrev.com/c/8160285. This CL removes the feature flag and cleans up the
now-obsolete `is_revalidating` plumbing across Blink, Mojo, and the
Network Service:

- Remove `features::kSafeRevalidation` and make structured revalidation
  metadata (`revalidation_etag` and `revalidation_last_modified`) the
  sole cache revalidation path.
- Remove `is_revalidating` from `network::mojom::URLRequest`,
  `network::ResourceRequest`, `blink::ResourceRequestHead`, and
  `blink::WebURLRequest`, and mark `PrefetchMatchesResourceRequestField`
  enum entry 39 as deprecated.
- Remove the `is_revalidating` parameter from
  `CorsUnsafeNotForbiddenRequestHeaderNames()`, `PreflightCache`, and
  `PreflightResult`.
- Add network::ResourceRequest::IsRevalidating() for the 304 CORS
  exemption, NetLogCorsURLLoaderStartParams(), and shared HTTP cache
  checks, and update PrefetchContainer validation to check
  revalidation_etag and revalidation_last_modified.

[ttc][android] Make the TTC voice plate's waveform follow the session

The large-screen voice plate's waveform now shows bars that follow the
microphone level while the session is live, including while the model
speaks, as on desktop. It shows resting dots otherwise: while
connecting, while the microphone is muted and while an error shows.

The session model gets WAVEFORM_MODE, which TtcSessionMediator works
out from the native session's lifecycle, so that a window that starts
showing a session shows its current state. The existing AUDIO_LEVEL now
also reaches the plate.

[HarfRust] Enable harfrust_capi by HarfRustShaping flag

Add the BackendTraits<true> specialization for harfrust_capi in
harfbuzz_face.cc, explicitly dispatching between variation-selector and
nominal glyph lookups in FontCallbacks::GetGlyph() because
hr_font_get_glyph() falls back to nominal lookup when a variation
selector is unmatched in cmap format 14.

Separate HarfBuzzFontCache maps, PooledHarfBuzzBuffer pools,
HarfBuzzFace instances, and LayoutLocale language handles by backend,
connect hb_shim::IsHarfRustEnabled() to
RuntimeEnabledFeatures::HarfRustShapingEnabled(), and add unit tests
covering runtime flag toggling.

Fuchsia-Binary-Size: Ditto.

During browser destruction, FindBarController destructor may be invoked
while web_contents() is still non-null, causing a CHECK failure dump.
This change reverts the CHECK macro back to DCHECK with a TODO comment
to exclude it from automatic conversion until the crash condition is
resolved.

0a3853b35b1a1a7ea1dd613e9d59cfa2d9ab0dc8 [ diff ] 
[Public Transit] Replace newTabFromMenu() with Station transitions

Replace ChromeTabUtils#newTabFromMenu() with Station transitions:
- TabGridIphTest opens the tab switcher from its Station and opens the
  new tab from RegularTabSwitcherStation.
- ContextualSearchInstrumentationBase starts on the test page with
  startOnTestServerUrl(), so ContextualSearchManagerTest and
  ContextualSearchSystemTest open tabs with openNewTabFast().
- MultiInstanceManagerApi31Test opens the tab from the new window's
  Station.
- TabUiTestHelper#createTabs(), createTabsWithThumbnail() and
  prepareTabsWithThumbnail() return the last new tab's Station (null if
  they opened none), and TabSwitcherTabletTest continues from it.
  Without a Station they hop on at the first new tab through a private
  helper.

New tabs use the default Trip timeout, like openNewTabFast(), instead
of newTabFromMenu()'s 10 seconds.

tree
97d3acc4d63cad3c73a81c3a9c37ac7057e3ca0c
parent
eb856bfa280437fb75149d24cebd32940e8dc749 [ diff ] 
[Settings] Stop disabling SettingsInTab in unit tests

SettingsInTab and SettingsInTabDesktop will be removed, so tests can no
longer turn them off. These Robolectric tests did so for reasons that
do not need the flags:

- MultiColumnTitleUpdaterTest and SettingsSearchCoordinatorUnitTest
  pass shownInTab=false to the class under test, which never reads the
  flags. The annotation is simply removed.
- SettingsNavigationImplTest, SettingsHostFragmentTest and
  ChromeActivityUnitTest need shouldOpenSettingsInTab() to be false.
  A narrow screen gives that. It is the Robolectric default, except in
  SettingsHostFragmentTest, which now asks for it.
- SettingsActivityUnitTest needs settings to open in SettingsActivity
  on wide screens too. It now calls
  SettingsInTab.setShouldOpenSettingsInTabForTesting(false).

Add Windows resource allowlist violations to no_check_generated_targets

Add the 61 grit targets that fail "gn check --check-generated" on
GPU Win x64 Builder, which sets enable_resource_allowlist_generation.

targets do not depend on it. Some of them cannot, since //ui/base
depends on them (e.g. //ui/strings:ui_strings, //net:net_resources).

6ea3d4ce6b24145e9975c08906de88754bbccc30 [ diff ] 
Reland "disk_cache: Use rename in POSIX MoveCache and check cleanup in RestartCache"

This is a reland of commit 13b9f13fabb4cbf0eb5a14df4d5e3a23e511804f.

The original change broke
CacheUtilTest.CleanupDirectorySucceedsWhenTargetDirectoryIsInaccessible
on macOS 13-15 CI builders. On macOS < 26, rename(2) returns EACCES on
a mode-0 directory, so the test previously passed only because
base::Move() fell back to CopyDirectory() and ignored the failed
deletion of the source directory. Update the test to make a child
directory inaccessible on macOS so MoveCache() can rename cache_dir_
while asynchronous deletion still encounters an inaccessible directory,
and assert that cache_dir_ is actually moved away on non-ChromeOS.

Original change's description:
> disk_cache: Use rename in POSIX MoveCache and check cleanup in RestartCache
>
> MoveDirectoryToTemporaryDirectory always moves a cache directory to a
> sibling path in the same parent directory, so cross-device copy
> fallback is unnecessary. Update POSIX disk_cache::MoveCache to validate
> the source and destination paths and use base::ReplaceFile instead of
> base::Move.
>
> Also update disk_cache::DeleteCache to return a boolean result and
> avoid enumerating non-directory paths when remove_folder is false, and
> update BackendImpl::RestartCache to check the cleanup result before
> calling SyncInit(), keeping the backend cleanly disabled if cleanup
> fails.
>
> Bug: 578691708

[webium] Wait for extension popup host in BrowserActionPopup

With `TopChromeWebUIPrerendering` enabled, `WebUIContentsPrerenderer`
creates a spare toolbar `WebContents` five seconds after the first
browser window opens. `BrowserActionInteractiveTest.BrowserActionPopup`
holds a process-wide `content::WebContentsAddedObserver` across
`WaitForExtensionsContainerLayout()` and `MainFrameSizeWaiter::Wait()`,
so that background refill trips `DCHECK(!web_contents_)` on slower
builds such as ChromeOS ASan and Linux debug.

Use `ExtensionHostTestHelper` restricted to
`mojom::ViewType::kExtensionPopup` and wait for
`WaitForRenderProcessReady()` instead, so the test only observes the
extension's popup host and preserves `MainFrameSizeWaiter` timing.

Bug: 525236301
Test: ./out/Default/interactive_ui_tests --gtest_filter=BrowserActionInteractiveTest.BrowserActionPopup --enable-features=InitialWebUI,WebUIReloadButton,TopChromeWebUISharedRenderer,TopChromeWebUIPrerendering:TopChromeWebUIPrerenderingUnbound/true --gtest_repeat=3

fd446b2f45dce539ea38d920f49911f917cbaed6
parent
c2dbb2db384cd47b3c896d3772a7093aefa615a4 [ diff ] 
[ui] Avoid mutating win32k dead-key state in ToUnicodeEx queries

PlatformKeyMap::UpdateLayout() and
DomKeyboardLayoutMapWin::GetDomKeyFromDomCodeForLayout() queried
Windows keyboard layouts via ToUnicodeEx() with wFlags=0 and then
injected VK_SPACE to clear any dead-key state. Because win32k dead-key
state is shared across processes on the same desktop, concurrent test
workers querying dead keys (such as on the French layout) raced and
corrupted subsequent ToUnicodeEx() translations, making
PlatformKeyMapTest.FRLayout flaky on Windows CI.

Pass bit 2 (0x04, do not change keyboard state, supported since
Windows 10 version 1607) in wFlags to ToUnicodeEx() and read the
dead-key combining character directly when ToUnicodeEx() returns -1,
removing the racy VK_SPACE follow-up calls.

Also filter out control characters (< 0x20) and lone UTF-16 surrogates
(!base::IsValidCodepoint) from 1-wchar_t ToUnicodeEx() results so they
do not produce non-printable DomKeys or ill-formed UTF-8 via
base::WriteUnicodeCharacter().

Test history:
https://ci.chromium.org/ui/test/chromium/%3A%2F%2Fui%2Fevents%5C%3Aevents_unittests%21gtest%3A%3APlatformKeyMapTest%23FRLayout

Bug: None

139c1f42370f0f99dba05cad8650fd93c91f6243 [ diff ] 
rgbkbd: Don't log an error when rgbkbd isn't installed

ChromeOS Flex is built without rgbkbd (USE=-rgbkbd), so
there is no D-Bus service file for org.chromium.Rgbkbd. At startup,
RgbKeyboardManager calls GetRgbKeyboardCapabilities, D-Bus fails right
away with org.freedesktop.DBus.Error.ServiceUnknown, and both
ObjectProxy and RgbKeyboardManager log an ERROR on every boot, even
though the device just has no RGB keyboard.

Switch the call to CallMethodWithErrorResponse(), which doesn't log on
its own, and report RgbKeyboardCapabilities::kNone for ServiceUnknown.
dbus-daemon only returns ServiceUnknown when no service file provides
the name, so real failures of an installed rgbkbd (e.g. it fails to
start or doesn't reply) still return std::nullopt and are still logged
as an ERROR, including the D-Bus error name.

On Flex, RgbKeyboardManager now records kNone to
ChromeOS.RgbKeyboard.RgbKeyboardCapabilityType, like other devices
without an RGB keyboard.

Also add unit tests for RgbkbdClient, which had none.

  /usr/share/dbus-1/system-services/ to imitate Flex and restart ui.
  Both ERROR logs are gone. With the file restored, the capabilities
  are still read from rgbkbd.

Bug: b:293273335
Test: chromeos_unittests --gtest_filter='RgbkbdClientTest.*'
Test: On a Corsola DUT, move org.chromium.Rgbkbd.service out of

parent
9619dffd73685fbe15366406a2b2fad75f1f17fa [ diff ] 
[TopChrome] Remove kInitialWebUIUseSeparateProcess

The InitialWebUI:use_separate_process param (off by default) was added
in https://crrev.com/c/7508984 as a kill switch that gives the Initial
WebUI a renderer process of its own, so that optimizations for its
process could be kept away from other Top Chrome WebUIs.

The param still works, but those optimizations (e.g. skipping extensions
and spellcheck setup) are keyed on
RenderProcessHost::IsForTopChromeWebUI(), so they apply to every Top
Chrome WebUI process whether or not the Initial WebUI is separate. So
the param no longer serves the purpose it was added for. A separate
process could have other benefits (e.g. a crash or heavy work in
another Top Chrome WebUI would not affect the Initial WebUI), but the
param was not added for them. The param only adds special cases to
site_info.cc and RenderProcessHostImpl::Init().

This CL removes the param and both special cases, also for the Android
tab strip new tab button, to which the param has applied since
https://crrev.com/c/8814213. The behavior with the default param value
does not change.

Tests that enabled the param now run with the default configuration;
InitialWebUINavigationBrowserTest.InitialWebUIProcessSharing now expects
the Initial WebUI to share a process with other Top Chrome WebUIs.

[ttc][android] Handle the TTC keyboard shortcuts

The keyboard shortcut and hold to speak shortcut saved on the TTC
settings page now work, on every form factor that has a keyboard,
including phones connected to a monitor:
- Keyboard shortcut (default Alt+Shift+K): starts a session, or ends
  the one that's running.
- Hold to speak shortcut (default Alt+Shift+Space): starts a session
  while it's held down and ends it when released. Holding it during a
  session that was already running leaves that session alone.

TtcShortcutHandlerAndroid registers the shortcuts from the
ttc.keyboard_shortcut and ttc.hold_to_speak_shortcut prefs with the
window's ui::AcceleratorManagerAndroid, the same way other customizable
shortcuts work on Android, and follows changes to the prefs. It reads
them with ttc::ParseShortcut(), so it only uses shortcuts the settings
page accepts. While the hold to speak shortcut is held, it also
registers that key's release. TtcSessionMediator owns one handler per
window, while sessions can run, and starts or ends sessions the same
way the toolbar button does.

Test: run_chrome_junit_tests -f 'org.chromium.chrome.browser.ttc.*'
Test: run_unit_tests --gtest_filter='TtcShortcutHandlerAndroid*'
Test: On a desktop Android emulator with --enable-features=Ttc,
  Alt+Shift+K starts a session, and holding Alt+Shift+Space starts one
  until the keys are released.

parent
04200d65b972e741ffd4c396bb4af397653ab255 [ diff ] 
3pp: Switch GraalVM native-image builds to Oracle GraalVM 25i

Oracle GraalVM 25i (Innovation on JDK 25 LTS) adds Native Image CRC32
intrinsics along with compiler and binary-size improvements.

Also raise the default native-image CPU target from -march=compatibility
to -march=x86-64-v2 -H:CPUFeatures=AVX,AES,CLMUL (the universal GCE
x86-64 baseline) so CRC32, CRC32C, POPCNT, AES, and AVX intrinsics are
enabled by default while AVX2 remains runtime-checked. Note that Linux
x86-64 build hosts running these binaries now require Intel Sandy
Bridge+ (or AMD Bulldozer+) CPUs with x86-64-v2, AVX, AES, and CLMUL.

Benchmarks (25.0.4 -> 25i6, idle-gated hyperfine):
- CrcBench (286 MB): 623.5 ms -> 99.2 ms (6.3x faster with CLMUL)
- signflinger (ChromePublic.apk): 339 ms -> 278 ms (-18%)
- chrome_r8 (ChromePublic.apk): 75.5 s -> 58.4 s (-23%), 132M -> 87M
- chrome_r8 --d8 (chrome_java): 1.89 s -> 1.74 s (-8%)

[Omnibox] Avoid capturing LocationBarMediator in reparent task

Capture local references to mLocationBarLayout and mDropdown in
LocationBarMediator.reparentToToolbar() instead of capturing
LocationBarMediator in the delayed PostTask runnable. This avoids
retaining the mediator across teardown while still allowing the
deferred removeView(mDropdown) cleanup to run after layout completes.

parent
b588b301c3b882c9c5517765967bdbbf69765024 [ diff ] 
[glic] Route Drive picker checks through GlicProviderSpec

Several call sites checked `features::kGlicDrivePicker` directly, which
bypasses `GlicProviderSpec`, so those checks ignored the profile's
provider (e.g. Gemini Enterprise).

* Move `kGlicDrivePicker` to `provider_features.h`. Per-profile checks
  now ask `SupportsFeature(GlicFeature::kDrivePicker)`: the shared WebUI
  check `DrivePickerUntrustedHostUIConfig::IsEnabled()` takes a
  `BrowserContext` and backs both configs' `IsWebUIEnabled()`
  (`DrivePickerHostUIConfig::IsEnabled()` forwards to it, since the
  untrusted target can't depend on the trusted one), and the picker
  controller checks the spec too.
* In `chrome_browser_interface_binders_webui_parts_desktop.cc`, remove
  the unused per-frame Drive picker binders (both WebUIs get their
  binders from `WebUIBrowserInterfaceBrokerRegistry`). The two broker
  registries, which are populated at startup with no profile, now
  register the Drive picker WebUIs unconditionally, like
  `SelectionOverlayUntrustedUI`: a broker is only created for a WebUI
  that exists, and creation is already gated by `IsWebUIEnabled()`.
* As a backstop, `BindInterface()` in both Drive picker WebUIs now
  reports a bad message and returns when the Drive picker is disabled
  for the profile.
* The WebUI check now returns false right away for non-regular profiles
  (e.g. incognito or guest). `DrivePickerHostController` resets a Glic
  style request to the default style when the profile's provider doesn't
  support the Glic Drive picker.

Since the spec's `kDrivePicker` check also requires
`omnibox::kDrivePickerV2Scope`, the Drive picker WebUIs are now enabled
only if AI Mode's `kComposeboxDriveContextMenuOption` is on, or the
profile's provider supports the Glic Drive picker. This changes behavior
for Gemini Enterprise profiles, and when `kDrivePickerV2Scope` is off
(it is on by default).

Fix stale downloader backends in Android test helper

The Android test helper could select a destroyed downloader or status
checker, causing callbacks to be dropped. Select active backends, verify
downloader parameters by feature, and report missing backends clearly.

Add tests for active-backend selection, feature-specific lookup, and
update test for callbacks after native destruction. This will support
the coming work tracked by crbug.com/585296918.

82fad492c9d32def224e28a3a9be5f857e80af54
parent
894f61fc4c5c97666a2e701ef80bc7e552243f57 [ diff ] 
[blink] Use simdutf direct encoding in TextEncoder::encode

Add unicode::CalculateUtf8Length and unicode::EncodeUtf8 in
wtf/text/utf8.* using simdutf (with scalar fallback using size_t and
U16_NEXT_OR_FFFD for unpaired UTF-16 surrogates) and gate
direct-to-buffer encoding in TextEncoder::encode behind
kTextEncoderDirectEncode (disabled by default).

Eliminating the 3x upfront std::string allocation and intermediate copy
reduces peak temporary memory on large mostly-ASCII inputs from ~4x to
1x and lets kThrowExceptionWhenTextEncodeOOM catch OOMs instead of
crashing in PartitionAlloc. Switching both the length pass and encoding
to simdutf also replaces the scalar U16_NEXT/U8_APPEND_UNSAFE loop with
vectorized transcoding, more than doubling throughput on long strings
despite the extra length pass.

Also adds long-string perf_tests for TextEncoder and TextDecoder.

Benchmark results (UNSCHEDULED_blink_perf.encoding,
text-encoder-long-strings.html, content_shell):
- TextEncoderDirectEncode OFF -> ON: 295.7 -> 655.9 runs/s (+122%)

Revert "[Linux/X11] Dispatch keyup when GTK filters dead-key release events"

This reverts commit 82bf08216cadf2718f35cd05c1ac93a4a0778742.

Reason for revert: Suspected cause of renderer CHECK failure
GetTextInputType() != ui::TEXT_INPUT_TYPE_NONE in
render_widget_host_view_aura.cc (crbug.com/590038161).

The revert resolves a ui/gtk/BUILD.gn conflict with commit
83a83ad0fc2c1 by keeping its x/gtk_ui_platform_x11_unittest.cc entry.

Original change's description:
> [Linux/X11] Dispatch keyup when GTK filters dead-key release events
>
> On X11 keyboard layouts with dead keys (such as pt-br, es, and
> us-intl), GtkIMContextSimple returns true for every GDK_KEY_RELEASE
> while a dead-key or Multi_key compose sequence is active, without
> re-injecting a fallback release event. InputMethodContextImplGtk
> reported those release events as handled, and InputMethodAuraLinux
> either dropped them (when TextInputType is NONE) or buffered them in
> ime_filtered_key_event_ without dispatching a DOM keyup event, leaving
> punctuation and modifier keys stuck pressed in web applications.
>
> In InputMethodContextImplGtk::DispatchKeyEvent(), only treat a GTK3
> KeyRelease as handled when an out-of-process IM module (IBus-GTK or
> Fcitx-GTK) sets kPropertyKeyboardImeHandledFlag on the event state
> (meaning the IME consumed the release or queued an async fallback
> release via gdk_event_put()), and never treat GTK4
> gtk_im_context_filter_key() release events as handled since GTK4 does
> not re-queue fallback X11 events.
>
> Fixed: 357905826
> Change-Id: I3353be69ad998aca787b8c2771c58484596bd10b

Reland "Android: Prune excess Java compile, ijar, and wrapper action deps"

This reverts commit fc432caf6baf98639c6645cadfbb80f6e16b593b.

Reason for reland: Depend on _android_sdk_dep on the top-level
java_library_impl group when proguard_enabled is set so ProGuard builds
still depend on SDK API database generation after pruning _non_java_deps
from prebuilt __header targets. Also update missing_symbol_test() to
copy Empty.java under target_gen_dir so compile_java/turbine still wait
for its helper group on clean builds.

Original change's description:
> Android: Prune excess Java compile, ijar, and wrapper action deps
>
> Tightens dependencies on Java compilation, prebuilt ijar, and
> standalone wrapper/generator actions to avoid waiting on unrelated
> non-Java targets, transitive __header deps, or additional_apks:
> - chrome_public_test_apk__apk_operations (Debug): 9,020 -> 14 steps
> - androidx_appcompat_appcompat_java__classes__ijar: 109 -> 4 steps
> - android_sdk_java__header (Debug): 4 -> 2 steps
>
> Bug: 579603584

parent
bdbda83521375a1fbb708b69248f65986890a15f [ diff ] 
[ozone] Apply scale changes to the software canvas without a resize

With software compositing on Wayland, changing the display scale from
1 to 1.5 while the browser window stays tiled or maximized makes the
window draw 1.5x too large, with blurry edges.

When the window keeps its size in pixels, SoftwareOutputDeviceOzone
skipped ResizeCanvas() and WaylandCanvasSurface ignored the new scale.
The buffers were then still committed with the old scale, so the
compositor scaled them up a second time. For tiled windows (e.g. on
Sway) this became visible once they lost their client-side border,
which used to change the pixel size on a scale change.

Forward scale changes to the canvas and let WaylandCanvasSurface take
the new scale even when the buffers can be kept.

Fixed: 589797299

7206a87e522144098b654a7b6660bbeaa5109cf8
parent
f8d6fbe7ff7b81b6eb7741690e878ffcea8fac54 [ diff ] 
[Linux] Apply high-contrast color recipes on USE_GTK builds

`ShouldApplyHighContrastColors()` previously returned false under
`BUILDFLAG(USE_GTK)`, assuming the Linux native mixer supplied
high-contrast colors like Windows. However,
`AddNativeChromeColorMixer()` on Linux does not override
`kColorOmniboxResults*` tokens, and `BUILDFLAG(USE_GTK)` is a
compile-time guard that also disabled high-contrast colors for the
default Classic (`SystemTheme::kDefault`) and Qt (`SystemTheme::kQt`)
themes. As a result,
`kColorOmniboxResultsBackgroundSelected` had only 1.12:1 to 1.37:1
contrast against `kColorOmniboxResultsBackground`.

Remove `BUILDFLAG(USE_GTK)` from `ShouldApplyHighContrastColors()`. For
Classic high contrast on Linux, this applies the shared high-contrast
omnibox, frame and toolbar recipes and skips the Material overrides in
`ShouldApplyChromeMaterialOverrides()`, matching other platforms.

GTK and Qt themes already override the toolbar and separator colors in
`AddNativeChromeColorMixer()`, but the hardcoded high-contrast frame
colors would replace the toolkit frame colors. Add
`kColorNativeFrameActive` and `kColorNativeFrameInactive`, set them in
the GTK and Qt mixers, and map them back to `kColorFrameActive` and
`kColorFrameInactive` in `AddNativeChromeColorMixer()` in high contrast.

Enable the `ColorProviderTest.OmniboxContrast` selected-row contrast
check on Linux.

Fixed: 40847971

[webium] Keep chrome.histograms metrics from top-chrome WebUIs

This CL keeps the metrics that `.top-chrome` WebUIs record through
`chrome.histograms` when `WebiumMetricsMapping` is enabled. The mapping
still filters Blink, V8 and cc histograms from those renderers, dropping
any that no built-in or served rule lists.

`chrome.histograms` now creates each histogram with a new flag,
`HistogramBase::kExemptFromNameMappingFlag`. Base passes each
histogram's flags to `NameMapper` and clears
`kExemptFromNameMappingFlag` after mapping. On both the shared-memory
and IPC merge paths, `MetricsNameMapper` keeps a flagged histogram under
its own name when no rule matches it, instead of dropping it. Matching
rules still apply, so a flagged histogram cannot bypass a built-in
rename.

The browser reads each histogram's flags as they were at creation, and
the `base::UmaHistogram*` helpers cannot pass extra flags. So the
binding shares three `WebUIHistogram*` helpers (ExactLinear,
CustomCounts, CustomTimes) and inlines the remaining single-caller
recordings into its lambdas, matching the `base::UmaHistogram*` layouts.

Histogram names do not change, so `histograms.xml` needs no update.

[support_tool] Migrate PII type sets to redaction::PIITypeSet

- Replace std::set<redaction::PIIType> with redaction::PIITypeSet in
  DataCollector::ExportCollectedDataWithPII and its overrides,
  SupportToolHandler, SupportPacketMetadata, the Support Tool WebUI and
  the fetch support packet remote command, passing sets by value.
- Use Has() and HasAll() instead of find(), count() and std::includes.
- Ignore missing or out-of-range PII type values received from the
  WebUI, since EnumSet::Put() CHECKs on out-of-range values.
- Update tests to match and cover invalid PII types from the WebUI.

970bac41946dc4c7fcec4317b290e2c8973e21d5
parent
14fc55b093206a1cb4a0ead3021a81aba6e6f37d [ diff ] 
[Permissions] Don't inherit embargo data into off-the-record profiles

PERMISSION_AUTOBLOCKER_DATA is registered as INHERIT_IN_INCOGNITO, so an
embargo recorded in a regular profile also applies in its off-the-record
profiles. A site can trigger an embargo without user interaction by
letting four notification prompts be ignored, then read it back from
incognito: permissions.query() returns "denied" instead of "prompt", and
Notification.requestPermission() is denied at once instead of after the
random 1-2 s delay NotificationPermissionContext uses to hide incognito.
With one origin per bit, this links an incognito session to the regular
profile.

Register PERMISSION_AUTOBLOCKER_DATA as DONT_INHERIT_IN_INCOGNITO, as
PERMISSION_AUTOREVOCATION_DATA already is. Each off-the-record profile
starts with no embargo data and keeps its own in memory.

[ttc][android] Record TTC keyboard shortcuts from the keyboard

The shortcut rows on the TTC settings page get an edit button. It opens
TtcShortcutRecorderDialog, which records a key press from the keyboard
and saves it as the row's shortcut. The dialog only saves a shortcut
that ttc::ValidateShortcut() accepts and that isn't TTC's other
shortcut, and says why it can't save any other.

A saved value that is no longer a valid shortcut, e.g. because Chrome
has started to use it, shows as Not set.

  chrome://settings/ai/ttc: Ctrl+T and Ctrl+Alt+K can't be saved, and
  Ctrl+Shift+Y is saved and shown in the row.

Add Mac failure expectation for content-visibility-with-popover-top-layer-005.html

This adds a Mac failure expectation for
content-visibility-with-popover-top-layer-005.html because there is
occasional horizontal jitter in the captured text (due to a subpixel
horizontal position difference, maybe related to layerization
differences).

This problem tends to show up more frequently on a few of the
rarely-used bots that are triggered by the WPT importer, which
interferes with WPT imports.

84b4049f869bf6f7a8e6a34778802f34715ed8a5 [ diff ] 
[Base] Add CoreAffinityPolicy and its eligibility check on Android

`SetCanRunOnBigCore()` can keep a thread off the big core cluster on
devices with at least 3 CPU core clusters. A follow-up CL adds the
opposite restriction, which keeps a thread off the little core cluster.

Add `base::CoreAffinityPolicy` to name the 3 sets of clusters that a
thread can be allowed on (`kLittleAndMidCores`, `kAllCores` and
`kMidAndBigCores`), and make the internal `GetAffinityMask()` compute
the mask of each of them. `IsEligibleForCoreAffinityPolicy()` returns
whether the device is eligible for a policy. For `kMidAndBigCores`,
this also requires at least 4 cores outside of the little core cluster.
Like `IsEligibleForBigCoreAffinityChange()`, it lets a caller check the
device before it queries a base::Feature.

The masks are cached per policy in `GetCachedAffinityMask()`, which
`IsEligibleForBigCoreAffinityChange()` and `SetCanRunOnBigCore()` now
use instead of their own static variables. Their behavior does not
change: the masks of `kAllCores` and `kLittleAndMidCores` are the ones
that `GetAffinityMask()` computed when `can_run` was true and false.

Design Doc (Google-internal):
https://docs.google.com/document/d/1O6Dp3yqK_ypj736GViJQF6bj1_AH8ovc_IGQubpDpAs/edit?resourcekey=0-yg6JBE8q_W5z2wrrBLUFtg
